It's just stayed low the whole time since then.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to that robbery, do you find yourself having unwanted memories or flashbacks about what happened, or do you actively try to avoid reminders of it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get those. Um... sometimes just hearing a cash box snap shut, or if somebody comes up behind me in a kitchen, it brings the robbery back so fast. It's like I can actually hear the voices again, and my stomach just drops. It feels exactly like it's happening right then.\n\nDoctor: When you had that robbery two years ago, did these feelings of not caring about things and the flashbacks start right away in the days or weeks after it happened, or did they develop more gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. The time right after it happened is kind of a blur. I don't remember exactly if the flashbacks and not caring about things started right away in those first few weeks, or if it sort of crept up on me later on. I just know it's been this way for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, in those first few weeks right after the robbery, were you still able to enjoy things like dance class or hiking, or had you already lost interest in those activities by then?\n\nPatient: Like I said, that time right after is a bit fuzzy, but... no, I don't think I was doing them then either. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in. I just barely care about the things that used to make me feel like myself. Dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, even game night... they all just sit there now like chores I keep putting off. I definitely wasn't enjoying them right after it happened, and I'm still not.\n\nDoctor: In those first few weeks right after the robbery, before you lost interest in your hobbies, were you able to feel okay mood-wise, or were you already feeling down and sad back then?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think I felt okay, no. Even back then, and... well, most days now, too. There's just this low, sour mix of fear and guilt under everything. It's hard to explain, but it's just always there in the background, even when nothing bad is actually going on.\n\nDoctor: In the very first week or two right after the robbery happened, before everything started feeling numb and low, do you remember feeling scared or shaken up, but still being able to feel other emotions or have moments where things felt somewhat normal?\n\nPatient: Um, I really don't know. Like I mentioned, those first couple of weeks are just a huge blur to me. I'm sure I was really scared and shaken up right after it happened, but... as for having normal moments or feeling other things? I honestly couldn't tell you. I just don't remember that time clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the very first day or two after the robbery, before things got blurry, do you remember if you were already feeling numb and losing interest in things, or did that numb, not-caring feeling come later—maybe weeks or months after the robbery happened?\n\nPatient: I think it started right away. This whole thing... the numbness and not caring about stuff... it's been going on since the robbery at that festival job. That was about two years and three months ago now. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure it didn't just show up later. It's been like this ever since it happened.\n\nDoctor: In those first few days right after the robbery, before things got blurry, were you able to sleep okay and eat normally, or were you already having trouble with those things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know. Like I said, those first few days are just... they're really blurry for me. I can't really remember how I was sleeping or eating right at the very beginning. I just don't have a clear memory of it.\n\nDoctor: Between the robbery happening and when you first started feeling this numbness and loss of interest, was there any time at all—even just a few days—when you felt more like yourself, before things shifted to how they are now?\n\nPatient: I don't think there was any time like that. It just feels like it's been this way ever since the robbery happened.\n\nDoctor: In the first day or two right after the robbery, before your memory gets blurry, do you remember what your mood was like—were you already feeling that low, sour mix of fear and guilt you described, or was your mood different back then?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I mentioned, those first couple of days are just a blur. I don't remember exactly how I was feeling right at the start, whether the fear and guilt were there immediately or not. I just don't have a clear picture of my mood from those first few days.\n\nDoctor: I know those first few days are blurry for you, but let me ask it differently: In the week or two right after the robbery, even if you don't remember every detail, do you recall whether you were able to do basic things like go to work, take care of yourself, or be around other people without much trouble—or were you already struggling with those kinds of everyday things right from the start?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I just don't know. It's just stayed low the whole time since then.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to that robbery, do you find yourself having unwanted memories or flashbacks about what happened, or do you actively try to avoid reminders of it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get those. Um... sometimes just hearing a cash box snap shut, or if somebody comes up behind me in a kitchen, it brings the robbery back so fast. It's like I can actually hear the voices again, and my stomach just drops. It feels exactly like it's happening right then.\n\nDoctor: When you had that robbery two years ago, did these feelings of not caring about things and the flashbacks start right away in the days or weeks after it happened, or did they develop more gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. The time right after it happened is kind of a blur. I don't remember exactly if the flashbacks and not caring about things started right away in those first few weeks, or if it sort of crept up on me later on. I just know it's been this way for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, in those first few weeks right after the robbery, were you still able to enjoy things like dance class or hiking, or had you already lost interest in those activities by then?\n\nPatient: Like I said, that time right after is a bit fuzzy, but... no, I don't think I was doing them then either. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in. I just barely care about the things that used to make me feel like myself. Dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, even game night... they all just sit there now like chores I keep putting off. I definitely wasn't enjoying them right after it happened, and I'm still not.\n\nDoctor: In those first few weeks right after the robbery, before you lost interest in your hobbies, were you able to feel okay mood-wise, or were you already feeling down and sad back then?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think I felt okay, no. Even back then, and... well, most days now, too. There's just this low, sour mix of fear and guilt under everything. It's hard to explain, but it's just always there in the background, even when nothing bad is actually going on.\n\nDoctor: In the very first week or two right after the robbery happened, before everything started feeling numb and low, do you remember feeling scared or shaken up, but still being able to feel other emotions or have moments where things felt somewhat normal?\n\nPatient: Um, I really don't know. Like I mentioned, those first couple of weeks are just a huge blur to me. I'm sure I was really scared and shaken up right after it happened, but... as for having normal moments or feeling other things? I honestly couldn't tell you. I just don't remember that time clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the very first day or two after the robbery, before things got blurry, do you remember if you were already feeling numb and losing interest in things, or did that numb, not-caring feeling come later—maybe weeks or months after the robbery happened?\n\nPatient: I think it started right away. This whole thing... the numbness and not caring about stuff... it's been going on since the robbery at that festival job. That was about two years and three months ago now. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure it didn't just show up later. It's been like this ever since it happened.\n\nDoctor: In those first few days right after the robbery, before things got blurry, were you able to sleep okay and eat normally, or were you already having trouble with those things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know. Like I said, those first few days are just... they're really blurry for me. I can't really remember how I was sleeping or eating right at the very beginning. I just don't have a clear memory of it.\n\nDoctor: Between the robbery happening and when you first started feeling this numbness and loss of interest, was there any time at all—even just a few days—when you felt more like yourself, before things shifted to how they are now?\n\nPatient: I don't think there was any time like that. I mean, like I said, I'm pretty sure the numbness started right away. And since that whole period right after is so blurry to me... I definitely don't remember having a few days where I felt like my old self. It just feels like it's been this way ever since the robbery happened.\n\nDoctor: In the first day or two right after the robbery, before your memory gets blurry, do you remember what your mood was like—were you already feeling that low, sour mix of fear and guilt you described, or was your mood different back then?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I mentioned, those first couple of days are just a blur. I don't remember exactly how I was feeling right at the start, whether the fear and guilt were there immediately or not. I just don't have a clear picture of my mood from those first few days.\n\nDoctor: I know those first few days are blurry for you, but let me ask it differently: In the week or two right after the robbery, even if you don't remember every detail, do you recall whether you were able to do basic things like go to work, take care of yourself, or be around other people without much trouble—or were you already struggling with those kinds of everyday things right from the start?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I just don't know. Like I keep saying, that whole time right after is just a big blank for me. I can't really tell you if I was going to work or, you know, doing normal things. I just don't remember how I was handling everyday stuff back then.\n\nDoctor: Even though those first days are blurry, do you remember having any days in that first month after the robbery when you felt scared or on edge about what happened, but you were still able to do things and weren't yet feeling this numb, low feeling you have now?\n\nPatient: I really wish I could give you a better answer, but I just don't know. That whole first month is pretty much gone from my memory. I don't remember having any days where I felt scared but could still get things done... or if the numbness had already started. It's just a blank space, so I really can't say if there was a time like that or not.\n\nDoctor: In those first few weeks after the robbery, even if you don't remember everything clearly, do you recall feeling jumpy, on high alert, or like you were constantly on edge—like your body was bracing for danger?\n\nPatient: I... I'm sorry, doctor, but I really just don't remember. It's like I told you, that whole time right after the robbery is just completely wiped from my mind. I don't know if I was jumpy or on edge back then because I just can't picture any of it. I wish I could tell you, but I just don't have those memories.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's like I just don't have any interest in them at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about things you used to enjoy? When did this start?\n\nPatient: Um, well... this has been going on since the robbery at that festival job I had. That was about two years and three months ago. Yeah, that's when this all started.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery two years ago, have you had any periods where your mood and interest in activities improved significantly, or has it been pretty consistently low and uninterested the whole time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's been pretty consistent, honestly. I haven't really had any periods where I suddenly felt like getting back into things. It's just stayed low the whole time since then.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to that robbery, do you find yourself having unwanted memories or flashbacks about what happened, or do you actively try to avoid reminders of it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get those. Um... sometimes just hearing a cash box snap shut, or if somebody comes up behind me in a kitchen, it brings the robbery back so fast. It's like I can actually hear the voices again, and my stomach just drops. It feels exactly like it's happening right then.\n\nDoctor: When you had that robbery two years ago, did these feelings of not caring about things and the flashbacks start right away in the days or weeks after it happened, or did they develop more gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. The time right after it happened is kind of a blur. I don't remember exactly if the flashbacks and not caring about things started right away in those first few weeks, or if it sort of crept up on me later on. I just know it's been this way for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, in those first few weeks right after the robbery, were you still able to enjoy things like dance class or hiking, or had you already lost interest in those activities by then?\n\nPatient: Like I said, that time right after is a bit fuzzy, but... no, I don't think I was doing them then either. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in. I just barely care about the things that used to make me feel like myself. Dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, even game night... they all just sit there now like chores I keep putting off. I definitely wasn't enjoying them right after it happened, and I'm still not.\n\nDoctor: In those first few weeks right after the robbery, before you lost interest in your hobbies, were you able to feel okay mood-wise, or were you already feeling down and sad back then?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think I felt okay, no. Even back then, and... well, most days now, too. There's just this low, sour mix of fear and guilt under everything. It's hard to explain, but it's just always there in the background, even when nothing bad is actually going on.\n\nDoctor: In the very first week or two right after the robbery happened, before everything started feeling numb and low, do you remember feeling scared or shaken up, but still being able to feel other emotions or have moments where things felt somewhat normal?\n\nPatient: Um, I really don't know. Like I mentioned, those first couple of weeks are just a huge blur to me. I'm sure I was really scared and shaken up right after it happened, but... as for having normal moments or feeling other things? I honestly couldn't tell you. I just don't remember that time clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the very first day or two after the robbery, before things got blurry, do you remember if you were already feeling numb and losing interest in things, or did that numb, not-caring feeling come later—maybe weeks or months after the robbery happened?\n\nPatient: I think it started right away. This whole thing... the numbness and not caring about stuff... it's been going on since the robbery at that festival job. That was about two years and three months ago now. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure it didn't just show up later. It's been like this ever since it happened.\n\nDoctor: In those first few days right after the robbery, before things got blurry, were you able to sleep okay and eat normally, or were you already having trouble with those things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know. Like I said, those first few days are just... they're really blurry for me. I can't really remember how I was sleeping or eating right at the very beginning. I just don't have a clear memory of it.\n\nDoctor: Between the robbery happening and when you first started feeling this numbness and loss of interest, was there any time at all—even just a few days—when you felt more like yourself, before things shifted to how they are now?\n\nPatient: I don't think there was any time like that. I mean, like I said, I'm pretty sure the numbness started right away. And since that whole period right after is so blurry to me... I definitely don't remember having a few days where I felt like my old self. It just feels like it's been this way ever since the robbery happened.\n\nDoctor: In the first day or two right after the robbery, before your memory gets blurry, do you remember what your mood was like—were you already feeling that low, sour mix of fear and guilt you described, or was your mood different back then?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I mentioned, those first couple of days are just a blur. I don't remember exactly how I was feeling right at the start, whether the fear and guilt were there immediately or not. I just don't have a clear picture of my mood from those first few days.\n\nDoctor: I know those first few days are blurry for you, but let me ask it differently: In the week or two right after the robbery, even if you don't remember every detail, do you recall whether you were able to do basic things like go to work, take care of yourself, or be around other people without much trouble—or were you already struggling with those kinds of everyday things right from the start?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I just don't know. Like I keep saying, that whole time right after is just a big blank for me. I can't really tell you if I was going to work or, you know, doing normal things. I just don't remember how I was handling everyday stuff back then.\n\nDoctor: Even though those first days are blurry, do you remember having any days in that first month after the robbery when you felt scared or on edge about what happened, but you were still able to do things and weren't yet feeling this numb, low feeling you have now?\n\nPatient: I really wish I could give you a better answer, but I just don't know. That whole first month is pretty much gone from my memory. I don't remember having any days where I felt scared but could still get things done... or if the numbness had already started. It's just a blank space, so I really can't say if there was a time like that or not.\n\nDoctor: In those first few weeks after the robbery, even if you don't remember everything clearly, do you recall feeling jumpy, on high alert, or like you were constantly on edge—like your body was bracing for danger?\n\nPatient: I... I'm sorry, doctor, but I really just don't remember. It's like I told you, that whole time right after the robbery is just completely wiped from my mind. I don't know if I was jumpy or on edge back then because I just can't picture any of it. I wish I could tell you, but I just don't have those memories.\n\nDoctor: In the first month after the robbery, did anyone around you—like family, friends, or coworkers—express concern about how you were doing, or did you reach out to anyone for help during that time?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know if anyone said anything to me back then. I mean, I guess they could have, but I just don't remember any conversations like that from that first month. I don't remember asking anyone for help, either. It's all just... missing. I'm sorry, I just can't remember.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. This whole thing... the numbness and not caring about stuff... it's been going on since the robbery at that festival job. That was about two years and three months ago now. It's been like this ever since it happened.\n\nDoctor: In those first few days right after the robbery, before things got blurry, were you able to sleep okay and eat normally, or were you already having trouble with those things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know. Like I mentioned, those first couple of days are just a blur. I don't remember exactly how I was feeling right at the start, whether the fear and guilt were there immediately or not. Like I keep saying, that whole time right after is just a big blank for me. I can't really tell you if I was going to work or, you know, doing normal things. I just don't remember how I was handling everyday stuff back then.\n\nDoctor: Even though those first days are blurry, do you remember having any days in that first month after the robbery when you felt scared or on edge about what happened, but you were still able to do things and weren't yet feeling this numb, low feeling you have now?\n\nPatient: I really wish I could give you a better answer, but I just don't know. That whole first month is pretty much gone from my memory. I don't remember having any days where I felt scared but could still get things done... or if the numbness had already started. It's just a blank space, so I really can't say if there was a time like that or not.\n\nDoctor: In those first few weeks after the robbery, even if you don't remember everything clearly, do you recall feeling jumpy, on high alert, or like you were constantly on edge—like your body was bracing for danger?\n\nPatient: I... I wish I could tell you, but I just don't have those memories.\n\nDoctor: In the first month after the robbery, did anyone around you—like family, friends, or coworkers—express concern about how you were doing, or did you reach out to anyone for help during that time?\n\nPatient: I...
